# HarnessGrad Agent Operating Rules

## Execution priorities

1. Close automatically solvable blockers and internalize reusable platform capabilities before launching large pilots.
2. Use isolated Codex worktrees only. Parent reviews every diff, runs focused tests, rejects stale reports and unrelated changes, then merges safe commits and updates the journal.
3. Keep up to 10 independent Codex tasks active when work can be parallelized. Every task must have a unique output filename; never trust inherited `reports/report.md` without checking the diff and commit.
4. Avoid idle rounds: combine waits with concrete receipt inspection, diff review, test execution, journal work or launch preparation. Do not spend a round only polling unchanged status.

## Working copies

There are exactly **two writers**, and the rule is about who yields to whom:

- **The operator writes directly to the main checkout** `/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/Study/HarnessGrad`
  whenever they want. That is supported, not a conflict. An agent merges **on top of** whatever
  it finds there and never rewrites, rebases away, reverts or moves an operator commit. When a
  merge conflicts because the operator changed the same file, the operator's change is the
  starting point and the agent's is the one that has to fit around it.
- **Agents never commit to `main`.** They work in registered `git worktree` checkouts under
  `/tmp/hg-*`, commit there, and the parent reviews the diff and merges.
- Every *other* copy of this repository on this machine is read-only or disposable.
- **Never create a full clone of this repository inside `Study/`.** Two were made on 2026-11-17
  (`HarnessGrad-appworld-controls-20261117`, `HarnessGrad-gaia-controls-20261117`). Both are now
  stranded on pre-rewrite commits that do not exist in `main`'s object store, and both still
  carry the old layout (`web/dist`, root-level report files). Their `origin` remotes -- which
  pointed at the main checkout, fetch **and push** -- have been removed, because a single
  `git push --force` from either would have restored the old history over `main`.
- They are kept, not deleted, and not reset: they hold run receipts and evidence
  (`gaia-evidence/`, `gaia-evidence-all17/`, `GAIA_FINAL_LAUNCH_RECEIPT.json`, the appworld
  `deliverables/` and `runtime/`) that exist nowhere else, and rule 5 of the staged pilot policy
  says old and incomplete receipts are preserved. Treat them as frozen evidence: read them, never
  fetch, merge, push or "update" them.
- 10 of 20 local branches also still point at pre-rewrite commits. They are refs, not working
  copies, so they are not a writing hazard, but they keep the old history reachable; decide
  explicitly before deleting any, since they are the only record of that codex work.
- If a copy is ever needed for an isolated run, make it a `git worktree` (registered, so
  `git worktree list` shows it) rather than a clone, and remove it when the run is done.

## Test execution

Pytest `--basetemp` must be **OUTSIDE the platform tree**, for example
`--basetemp=/tmp/harnessgrad-pytest-quality`. Driver-based tests use `tmp_path`
as a harness work root, which the runner correctly refuses inside the platform.
Omitting the option preserves pytest's normal default.

## GPU execution

- **One local endpoint: `http://127.0.0.1:8002/v1`** (model `qwen3.5-9b-local`). GPU0 and the
  `8001` server were released on 2026-10-14 to give a card back; do not start a second one.
- `eval/unix_model_relay.py` hard-codes the agent model route to `127.0.0.1:8002`, so `8002` is
  the endpoint a containerised harness actually reaches. That, not preference, is why this is
  the one that was kept -- the other would have needed a production change.
- Keep the endpoint productively occupied with necessary no-model controls, diagnostics or valid
  pilots; never create duplicate, fake or scientifically unjustified work for utilization. If
  there is no approved diagnostic or pilot, an idle loaded server is the correct state: record
  that rather than inventing work.
- Bind each run to one endpoint and one fresh run ID. Keep total H0 concurrency `<=3`, `jobs=1`.

## Staged pilot policy

- First run a one-task/one-trial diagnostic after all dataset gates pass.
- For temporary reruns of blocked/incomplete datasets, select `min(30, ceil(10% * eligible))` tasks before execution, with `trials=1`, `jobs=1`.
- Expand only after a separate review of a valid measured diagnostic receipt. Do not silently revert to the historical `min(50, ceil(10% * eligible)) x 3` protocol.
- Invalid, unmeasured, timeout, scorer, provider, cleanup or snapshot failures are not ordinary zero scores.
- Preserve old and incomplete receipts; never resume or overwrite an old run ID.

## Dataset taxonomy

Task capability is a cross-table: `demand x surface`. Harness coverage is a separate axis: `exercises`. Do not collapse these into one flat dataset category.

## Prohibited shortcuts

No method tuning, paid endpoints, heldout leakage, SOTUQA, API-Bank, NESTFUL, Tau2-bench, Matrix, score fabrication or fake benchmark utilization.

## Evidence requirements

Every diagnostic or pilot records metric identity, eligible/selected/task/trial coverage, invalid and unmeasured counts, source/split/manifest/model/harness hashes, endpoint binding, resource/budget limits, cleanup receipt and comparability notes.

## Blocker closure priorities

Prioritize selected-only/privacy and provenance, scorer dependency/network and offline closure, trusted verifier/runtime, artifact-only state export, filesystem quota enforcement, official scorer execution, snapshot lifecycle and cleanup. A passing synthetic control is not a production H0 authorization.

# Withdrawn requirements: proven-unobtainable evidence becomes a recorded limit

Operator decision, 2026-10-15. This file is the evidence record for every blocker
requirement withdrawn under this decision. It follows the same standard the operator
already applied to `paired-filesystem-quotas`, `snapshot-owned-cleanup` and
`official-scorer-identity`: **a requirement that cannot be satisfied here is recorded as a
limit with the search evidence attached, rather than left open forever.**

A requirement withdrawn here is one that no amount of further work in this repository can
satisfy — not one that is merely difficult. Each entry names what was searched and what
came back empty or impossible.

The operator's further decision on 2026-10-15 extends this record to the three runtime
requirements below. Their evidence describes why they cannot be advanced in the current
scope; withdrawal does not assert that a future provisioned runtime could never be tested.
No new live run, receipt authentication or search result is claimed by these entries.

## Shared requirement withdrawn: `scorer-offline-closure`

The withdrawn shared requirement includes S6 "validate live controls before pilots".
The remaining clauses retain their bounded evidence and residual limits; withdrawal
does not certify complete transitive image/ABI closure or official metric equivalence.

**Why it cannot be satisfied by work.** The clause asks for live controls whose staged
receipts are *cleared*, but the observation flag that would clear them is hard-coded false:
`w-s6` traced the path and showed the flag only flips after the admission the clause itself
helps withhold. It is a cycle, not a gap.

**What is retained instead.** The control *evidence* exists and stays in the record at its
actual scope — real raw results, each still publishing `score=null`:

| class | control | raw result |
|---|---|---|
| actor-workdir | ALFWorld noop | reward 0.0 |
| snapshot-service | Terminal-Bench `openssl-selfsigned-cert` | reward 0.0, CTRF 5/6 passed |
| in-process | ChessBench stepwise | terminal exchange, controller 0.0 |
| pristine-offline | pristine control | measured 0.0 (answer 41 vs expected 42) |
| mounted-service | AppWorld | measured loss |
| JVM / LibreOffice / child-process | ScienceWorld, SpreadsheetBench, DS-1000, LiveCodeBench, BIRD | lifecycle/limit/cleanup observations |

Terminal-Bench verifier coverage reached 13/89 real measurements; the 45th-round live
control set is preserved in the journal and the run artifacts.

## Shared requirement withdrawn: `artifact-only-state-export`

**Searched:** `w-scope` reviewed all 28 declared datasets and the QA scaffold for
pristine applicability; `w-quiesce2`, `w-e5`, `w-wal` and `w-e6b` examined selected-file
custody, scoring equivalence and lifecycle observations at their recorded scopes.
**Result:** zero pristine-offline declarations or selections. E1/E2/E3 are
SATISFIED FOR THE CURRENT DATASET SET (zero applicable pristine datasets), but
no real-dataset pristine export, private-input safety or backend ownership validation is established.
Selected-file endpoint probes do not establish interval quiescence: the same-UID
directory-substitution/restore counterexample evades those probes. Main-only SQLite
export omitted committed WAL state and changed the authored BIRD EX result from 0 to 1;
the subsequent native bound-input path refuses WAL-format SQLite before scoring.
E4/E5/E6 remain PARTIAL as standing limits, rather than a claim of general equivalence
or complete lifecycle coverage. This records the operator's withdrawal; it does not
classify every unexercised branch as impossible to test.

**Retained future opt-in boundary:** Every future pristine opt-in must provision authoritative per-task export
and selected private-input/gold contracts, and validate actual backend/source/projection ownership.
The former next_action remains the scope of that work, not an active shared blocker:

> Provision authoritative per-task export and private-input contracts for pristine scorers;

locator `## Exact registry wording`; its carrier was the withdrawn shared blocker.

## Dataset requirements withdrawn as unobtainable

### `livecodebench / row-attestation`
**Asked for:** a metadata-only complete upstream catalogue/rank plus authenticated
row/pool/split/statement linkage.
**Searched:** the authoritative upstream source, repeatedly, across three separate rounds.
**Result:** **zero authoritative upstream row IDs obtainable**; the upstream count is not
published, so the locally declared 1,055 count cannot be corroborated from public sources.
Six pinned shard identities were linked to the adapter's own declarations, and the inert
selection and conditional split reproduce — that part is retained.

### `twowikimultihopqa / hub-data-provenance`
**Former family:** `source-provenance`.
**Asked for:** metadata-only original-release archive/file digest custody and completeness
attestation.
**Searched:** exact-revision upload history, release/tag inventories, a zero-result
licence-issue search, and the original and later Dropbox HEAD receipts.
**Result:** the original release is advertised with an **opaque ETag and no content digest**
(a 259,216,602-byte attachment). An opaque ETag is not a digest and was explicitly refused
as a substitute. The paper claims 192,606 examples while the witness card carries
conflicting counts, so no coverage attestation can be derived either.

### `twowikimultihopqa / ujson-native-offline-closure`
**Former family:** `scorer-dependency-network`.
**Asked for:** authenticated linkage from the published wheel to its source/build inputs,
including the double-conversion build selection.
**Searched:** PyPI build attestation (**404, "No provenance available"**) and then a full
**reproducible rebuild** from the pinned commit `59875e08` inside the pinned image.
**Result:** the rebuild **did not reproduce** — all six member names match and LICENSE,
METADATA and `top_level` are byte-identical, but the compiled extension, `WHEEL` and
`RECORD` differ. The embedded double-conversion implementation is confirmed present; the
original vendored-versus-external-static selection is not observable in the artifact. The
image-bound runtime closure that *is* obtainable (network-denied import, resolved loader and
symbol providers, synthetic evaluator) was completed and retained.

### `bird_minidev / sql-coverage-reconciliation`
**Asked for:** original selected-ID/phase/trial/error metadata binding the 90 phases to
actual measurements.
**Searched:** the tracked-source inventory.
**Result:** only summaries exist; the original phase/trial/error ledger is **not present in
this repository** and is not published upstream. The v4 incomplete record,
the v5 historical complete label and the partial-measurement/effect=null state are preserved
rather than reinterpreted.

### `ds1000 / host-slice-archive-review`
**Asked for:** original selected-ID/split/host-pool provenance plus original trial outcomes
and timeout/error/lifecycle records.
**Searched:** the same inventory.
**Result:** those records **do not exist in the repository**; `ds1000-v4-pending-review` is
preserved with `effect=null`. Archive acceptance stays blocked in the sense that no
measurement is claimed from it — what is withdrawn is the requirement to *produce* records
that were never captured.

     1	r"""HotpotQA fullwiki validation adapter using the official joint metric.
     2	
     3	`prediction.json` carries the answer and optional `(title, sentence_index)` pairs.
     4	Answer EM/F1 and supporting-fact scores feed the official joint EM/F1; support facts use
     5	exact set semantics. Missing evidence remains a valid prediction and produces joint zero.
     6	The adapter reads only the pinned validation split, not test gold.
     7	"""
     8	from __future__ import annotations
     9	
    10	from data.scaffolds.qa import base
    11	from data.scaffolds.qa.evidence_scorers import (hotpot_exact_match, hotpot_f1_score,
    12	                                      SpScore, hotpot_joint, hotpot_update_sp, MissingEvidence)
    13	
    14	KIND = "dataset"
    15	
    16	DATASET_ROOT = base.Path("/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/datasets/hotpotqa")
    17	REVISION = "1908d6afbbead072334abe2965f91bd2709910ab"
    18	CONFIG = "fullwiki"
    19	VALIDATION = f"{CONFIG}/validation-00000-of-00001.parquet"
    20	TEST = f"{CONFIG}/test-00000-of-00001.parquet"
    21	
    22	
    23	def _gold(row: dict) -> dict:
    24	    facts = row.get("supporting_facts")
    25	    if isinstance(facts, dict):
    26	        titles = facts.get("title") or []
    27	        indices = facts.get("sent_id") or []
    28	        if len(titles) != len(indices):
    29	            raise ValueError("hotpotqa: supporting_facts title/sent_id lengths differ")
    30	        facts = [[title, index] for title, index in zip(titles, indices)]
    31	    return {"answer": str(row["answer"]), "supporting_facts": facts}
    32	
    33	EVIDENCE_AWARE = True
    34	ORACLE_SOURCE = "released"
    35	
    36	
    37	def score_prediction(prediction: dict, gold: dict) -> dict:
    38	    answer = prediction["answer"]
    39	    em = hotpot_exact_match(answer, gold["answer"])
    40	    f1, precision, recall = hotpot_f1_score(answer, gold["answer"])
    41	    try:
    42	        sp = hotpot_update_sp(prediction.get("predicted_evidence"),
    43	                              gold.get("supporting_facts"))
    44	    except MissingEvidence:
    45	        sp = SpScore(em=0.0, f1=0.0, prec=0.0, recall=0.0)
    46	    joint_em, joint_f1 = hotpot_joint(em, precision, recall, sp)
    47	    return {"reward": joint_em, "correct": bool(joint_em == 1.0),
    48	            "official": {"metric": "HotpotQA joint", "em": em, "f1": f1,
    49	                          "sp_em": sp.em, "sp_f1": sp.f1,
    50	                          "joint_em": joint_em, "joint_f1": joint_f1},
    51	            "why": f"joint_em={joint_em:g}, joint_f1={joint_f1:g}"}
    52	
    53	
    54	def score(prediction: str, gold: dict) -> dict:
    55	    return score_prediction({"answer": prediction}, gold)
    56	
    57	
    58	def _rows() -> list[dict]:
    59	    path = DATASET_ROOT / VALIDATION
    60	    if not path.is_file():
    61	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"no {VALIDATION} at {path}")
    62	    return base.parquet_rows(path)
    63	
    64

    71	    goal=lambda question, gold: (
    72	        f"Question: {question}\n\nWrite the answer to `answer.txt` with `qa submit`, or "

    74	        "{\"schema_version\":1,\"answer\":\"...\",\"predicted_evidence\":["

    76	        "required for a nonzero joint score."),

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Validate the source-only blocker index; never import adapters or open receipts."""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import ast
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
REGISTRY = ROOT / "registry/dataset-blockers.json"
HISTORY = ROOT / "tests/fixtures/dataset_blockers/history-codex178.json"
# Pin the review baseline independently of the editable registry.
HISTORY_SHA256 = "4c778d435f4147eb04dbcee4e37a2bac3f0ac5a55584e3adf252ab12972d1300"
STATUSES = {"code-solvable", "external", "runtime", "measurement"}
FAMILIES = {
    "selected-only-privacy", "source-provenance", "scorer-dependency-network",
    "trusted-verifier-runtime", "artifact-only-export", "filesystem-quota",
    "official-scorer-execution", "snapshot-cleanup", "measurement-coverage",
    "metric-comparability", "platform-integrity", "actor-protocol",
    "termination-audit", "contract-refusal", "journal-coverage", "documentation-conflict",
}


class RegistryError(ValueError):
    pass


def _require(condition, message: str) -> None:
    if not condition:
        raise RegistryError(message)


def _unique_object(pairs):
    obj = {}
    for key, value in pairs:
        _require(key not in obj, f"duplicate JSON key: {key}")
        obj[key] = value
    return obj


def _reject_constant(value):
    raise RegistryError(f"nonfinite JSON constant: {value}")


def read_json(path: Path):
    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"),
                      object_pairs_hook=_unique_object, parse_constant=_reject_constant)


def literal_declarations(path: Path, names: set[str]) -> dict:
    """Read only module-level literals, without running even taxonomy imports."""
    found = {}
    for node in ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), filename=str(path)).body:
        if isinstance(node, ast.Assign):
            targets = node.targets
        elif isinstance(node, ast.AnnAssign):
            targets = [node.target]
        else:
            continue
        for target in targets:
            if isinstance(target, ast.Name) and target.id in names:
                _require(target.id not in found, f"{path.name}: repeated {target.id}")
                try:
                    found[target.id] = ast.literal_eval(node.value)
                except (ValueError, TypeError) as exc:
                    raise RegistryError(f"{path.name}: {target.id} must be a literal") from exc
    return found


def published_declarations(root: Path = ROOT) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """Conservative chooser coverage, including unprovisioned/refused datasets.

    `data.scaffolds.registry.published()` imports adapters and may omit an import failure.
    Reading KIND declarations covers every publishable source without loading a
    mixed task pool. A missing literal KIND retains the registry's fixture default.
    """
    return tuple(path.stem for path in sorted((root / "data" / "datasets").glob("*.py"))
                 if literal_declarations(path, {"KIND"}).get("KIND") == "dataset")


def _fields(obj, expected: set[str], label: str) -> None:
    _require(isinstance(obj, dict), f"{label}: expected object")
    _require(set(obj) == expected, f"{label}: fields must be {sorted(expected)}")


def _text(value, label: str) -> None:
    _require(isinstance(value, str) and bool(value.strip()), f"{label}: empty text")


def _unique_strings(values, label: str) -> None:
    _require(isinstance(values, list), f"{label}: expected list")
    for value in values:
        _text(value, label)
    _require(len(set(values)) == len(values), f"{label}: duplicates")


def _evidence(items, root: Path, label: str) -> None:
    _require(isinstance(items, list) and bool(items), f"{label}: evidence required")
    kinds = []
    for item in items:
        _fields(item, {"kind", "path", "locator"}, label)
        kind, path = item["kind"], item["path"]
        _text(path, label)
        _text(item["locator"], label)
        _require(kind in {"repository", "external-reference"}, f"{label}: evidence kind")
        kinds.append(kind)
        if kind == "external-reference":
            # This is a historical citation, not a file we have verified or may open.
            _require(Path(path).is_absolute(), f"{label}: external reference must be absolute")
            continue
        rel = Path(path)
        _require(not rel.is_absolute() and ".." not in rel.parts,
                 f"{label}: repository evidence must stay inside root")
        source = root / rel
        _require(source.resolve().is_relative_to(root.resolve()), f"{label}: evidence escapes root")
        _require(source.is_file(), f"{label}: missing evidence {path}")
        _require(item["locator"] in source.read_text(encoding="utf-8"),
                 f"{label}: stale evidence locator in {path}")
    _require("repository" in kinds, f"{label}: repository observation required")


def _blocker(blocker, root: Path, label: str) -> None:
    _fields(blocker, {"id", "family", "status", "observed", "evidence", "next_action"}, label)
    for field in ("id", "observed", "next_action"):
        _text(blocker[field], f"{label}.{field}")
    _require(blocker["family"] in FAMILIES, f"{label}: unknown blocker family")
    _require(blocker["status"] in STATUSES, f"{label}: unknown status")
    _evidence(blocker["evidence"], root, label)


def validate(document: dict, *, root: Path = ROOT) -> dict:
    _fields(document, {"schema_version", "review", "shared_blockers", "datasets"}, "registry")
    _require(type(document["schema_version"]) is int and document["schema_version"] == 1,
             "unsupported schema version")
    _fields(document["review"], {"date", "scope", "production_authorized", "notes"}, "review")
    _text(document["review"]["date"], "review.date")
    _require(document["review"]["scope"] == "repository-source-and-journal-only", "review scope")
    _require(document["review"]["production_authorized"] is False, "registry cannot authorize production")
    _unique_strings(document["review"]["notes"], "review.notes")

    taxonomy = literal_declarations(root / "tools/dataset_classes.py", {"CAPABILITY", "EXERCISES"})
    published = set(published_declarations(root))
    _require(set(taxonomy["CAPABILITY"]) == published, "taxonomy/published declarations differ")
    _require(set(taxonomy["EXERCISES"]) == published, "exercise/published declarations differ")
    _require(isinstance(document["shared_blockers"], list), "shared_blockers: expected list")
    shared = set()
    for blocker in document["shared_blockers"]:
        _blocker(blocker, root, "shared blocker")
        _require(blocker["id"] not in shared, "duplicate shared blocker")
        shared.add(blocker["id"])

    _require(isinstance(document["datasets"], list), "datasets: expected list")
    seen, history, run_ids = set(), {}, set()
    for row in document["datasets"]:
        _fields(row, {"dataset", "demand", "surface", "exercises", "shared_blockers",
                      "blockers", "historical_runs"}, "dataset")
        name = row["dataset"]
        _text(name, "dataset name")
        _require(name not in seen, f"duplicate dataset: {name}")
        _require(name in published, f"unpublished dataset: {name}")
        seen.add(name)
        _require((row["demand"], row["surface"]) == taxonomy["CAPABILITY"][name],
                 f"{name}: demand x surface drift")
        _unique_strings(row["exercises"], f"{name}.exercises")
        _require(row["exercises"] == list(taxonomy["EXERCISES"][name]), f"{name}: exercise drift")
        _unique_strings(row["shared_blockers"], f"{name}.shared_blockers")
        _require(set(row["shared_blockers"]) <= shared, f"{name}: unknown shared blocker")
        # An empty `blockers` list is allowed, and is a statement rather than a blank: it
        # says every requirement this dataset carried was reviewed and either met or
        # withdrawn by decision. `_fields` above already requires the key to be present, so
        # an accidentally-omitted list is still caught; what the old `bool(...)` added was
        # only the inability to express "cleared".
        #
        # This is deliberately NOT the same move as adding a value to `STATUSES` so a
        # finished blocker can stay parked in the registry with a status meaning "done".
        # That keeps a closed requirement counted as live and widens a closed vocabulary;
        # this removes a non-emptiness check so closure has somewhere to land. A dataset
        # cleared this way must have its decision recorded in a checked-in document, and
        # `scienceworld` is the case: see `docs/enforcement-proof-decision.md`.
        _require(isinstance(row["blockers"], list),
                 f"{name}: blockers must be a list")
        ids = set()
        for blocker in row["blockers"]:
            _blocker(blocker, root, name)
            _require(blocker["id"] not in ids, f"{name}: duplicate blocker")
            ids.add(blocker["id"])
        _require(isinstance(row["historical_runs"], list), f"{name}: history must be a list")
        for run in row["historical_runs"]:
            _fields(run, {"record_id", "run_id", "status", "effect", "observation", "evidence"}, name)
            _text(run["record_id"], f"{name}: historical record ID")
            if run["run_id"] is not None:
                _text(run["run_id"], f"{name}: run ID")
                _require(run["run_id"] not in run_ids, f"{name}: duplicate historical run ID")
                run_ids.add(run["run_id"])
            _require(run["status"] in {"incomplete", "partial-measurement", "pending-review"},
                     f"{name}: historical incomplete cannot become complete")
            _require(run["effect"] is None, f"{name}: historical incomplete cannot acquire an effect")
            _text(run["observation"], f"{name}: historical observation")
            _evidence(run["evidence"], root, f"{name}: historical evidence")
            key = run["record_id"]
            _require(key not in history, f"duplicate historical record: {key}")
            history[key] = {"dataset": name, "record": run}
    _require(seen == published, f"missing published datasets: {sorted(published - seen)}")

    raw = HISTORY.read_bytes()
    _require(hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest() == HISTORY_SHA256, "historical baseline pin changed")
    baseline = json.loads(raw, object_pairs_hook=_unique_object, parse_constant=_reject_constant)
    for old in baseline["records"]:
        key = old["record"]["record_id"]
        _require(history.get(key) == old, f"historical record removed or changed: {key}")
    return {"datasets": len(seen), "shared_blockers": len(shared), "historical_records": len(history)}


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument("--registry", type=Path, default=REGISTRY)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    try:
        counts = validate(read_json(args.registry))
    except (RegistryError, OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as exc:
        print(json.dumps({"valid": False, "error": str(exc)}))
        return 2
    print(json.dumps({"valid": True, **counts, "production_authorized": False}))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

    19	    return p
